🆓 Rune Etch Orders are **free for Xverse API customers**. For free trial users, Xverse API charges a 2,000 sats fee for every Rune Etch order.

Create Rune Etch Order - Request[](https://docs.xverse.app/api/runes/etch-rune/create-rune-etch-order#create-rune-etch-order-request)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

You can specify the characteristics of the Runes Etch order you wish to execute, by passing a request body, with the below properties:

RunesEtchParams Property

Description

`runeName`

a string representing the [name of the Rune](https://docs.ordinals.com/runes.html#name) to etch

`divisibility` (ℹ️ optional)

a number representing the rune's [divisibility](https://docs.ordinals.com/runes.html#divisibility)
, i.e. how finely it may be divided into its atomic units. Divisibility is expressed as the number of digits permissible after the decimal point in an amount of runes. The default value is 0.

`symbol` (ℹ️ optional)

a string representing the rune's currency [symbol](https://docs.ordinals.com/runes.html#symbol)
 as a single Unicode code point. If a rune does not have a symbol, the generic currency sign `¤`, also called a scarab, should be used

`premine` (ℹ️ optional)

a string representing rune [premine](https://docs.ordinals.com/runes.html#premine)
: the amount of rune units which the etcher can optionally allocate to themselves.

`isMintable`

a boolean indicating if the rune may have an open mint, allowing anyone to create and allocate units of that rune for themselves.

`terms` (ℹ️ optional)

an object describing the [terms](https://docs.ordinals.com/runes.html#terms)
 governing minting if the rune is mintable, with the below properties:

*   `amount` a string representing the fixed amount of new units of the rune that each mint transaction creates
    
*   `cap` a string representing the number of times a rune may be minted.
    
*   `heightStart` the mint is open starting in the block with the given start height. ℹ️Optional
    
*   `heightEnd` the rune may not be minted in or after the block with the given end height. ℹ️Optional
    
*   `offsetStart` the mint is open starting in the block whose height is equal to the start offset plus the height of the block in which the rune was etched. ℹ️Optional
    
*   `offsetEnd` the rune may not be minted in or after the block whose height is equal to the end offset plus the height of the block in which the rune was etched. ℹ️Optional
    

`inscriptionDetails` (ℹ️ optional)

You can optionally use the Rune etching transactions to **create an inscription**: - the etched Runes will be **linked** to the inscription, which marketplaces and apps can use as logo - the inscription will be sent to the specified `destinationAddress`, along with the premined Runes If you wish to use that option, pass an `inscriptionDetails` object with the below properties to specify the content to inscribe:

*   `contentType` a string representing the content/mime type of the content being inscribed. Make sure you extract and pass the correct content type for the content you wish to inscribe
    
*   `contentBase64` a string representing the content you wish to inscribe, encoded in Base64 format
    

`delegateInscriptionId` (ℹ️ optional)

If you wish to use the Rune etching transactions to create an inscription, you can optionally nominate a [delegate](https://docs.ordinals.com/inscriptions/delegate.html)
 for your inscription, as an **alternative** to specifying its content with `inscriptionDetail` (see above)

You can **instead** choose to pass a `delegateInscriptionId` string representing the inscription ID of the delegate.

`turbo` (ℹ️ optional)

a boolean marking the rune etching as opting into future rune protocol changes if set to `true` According to the [Runes specifications](https://docs.ordinals.com/runes/specification.html)
: _These protocol changes may increase light client validation costs, or just be highly degenerate_ ℹ️the default value is `false`

`destinationAddress`

The Bitcoin address to which the minted runes should be allocated. ⚠️ When minting Runes for your user, we recommend managing runes holdings on taproot/ordinal address.

`feeRate`

a number representing the desired fee rate to set for the Rune etch transactions, in sats per vbytes

`appServiceFee` (ℹ️ optional)

a number representing the sats value of the fee to charge your user for your service.

`appServiceFeeAddress` (ℹ️ optional)

a string representing the Bitcoin address which will receive the `appServiceFee`, if a fee is specified

`refundAddress`

The Bitcoin address to which the amount of the funding transaction should be returned, should the etch order fail. ⚠️ When etching Runes for your user, we recommend managing refunds on their Bitcoin **payment** address.

`network` (ℹ️ optional)

a string representing the Bitcoin network to use for the etch transactions: `'Mainnet'` or `'Testnet'`

Create Rune Etch Order - Response[](https://docs.xverse.app/api/runes/etch-rune/create-rune-etch-order#create-rune-etch-order-response)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

A successful Create Etch Order request will:

1.  Create a Runes Etch order for the Xverse API to process, identified by an `orderId`
    
2.  Return a the below response object to your app:
    

Create Etch Order - Response

Description

`orderId`

a string representing the ID of the Rune Etch order created by the Xverse API

`fundAddress`

the BTC address which will collect the funds required to [execute the Rune Etch order](https://docs.xverse.app/api/runes/etch-rune/execute-rune-etch-order)
, and sign the required transactions to process the order.

`fundAmount`

the amount of sats to send to `fundAddress` the before you [execute the Rune Etch order](https://docs.xverse.app/api/runes/etch-rune/execute-rune-etch-order)

Next Step -> Fund & Execute Etch Order[](https://docs.xverse.app/api/runes/etch-rune/create-rune-etch-order#next-step-greater-than-fund-and-execute-etch-order)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Once you've funded your Rune Etch order and sent the required `fundAmount` to the `fundAddress` , you can call the [Execute Etch Order](https://docs.xverse.app/api/runes/etch-rune/execute-rune-etch-order)
 endpoint.

[PreviousEstimate Rune Etch Order](https://docs.xverse.app/api/runes/etch-rune/estimate-rune-etch-order)
[NextExecute Rune Etch Order](https://docs.xverse.app/api/runes/etch-rune/execute-rune-etch-order)

Last updated 10 months ago

*   [Quick Start](https://docs.xverse.app/api/runes/etch-rune/create-rune-etch-order#quick-start)
    
*   [post/v1/runes-managed/etch/orders](https://docs.xverse.app/api/runes/etch-rune/create-rune-etch-order#post-v1-runes-managed-etch-orders)
    
*   [Create Rune Etch Order - Request](https://docs.xverse.app/api/runes/etch-rune/create-rune-etch-order#create-rune-etch-order-request)
    
*   [Create Rune Etch Order - Response](https://docs.xverse.app/api/runes/etch-rune/create-rune-etch-order#create-rune-etch-order-response)
    
*   [Next Step -> Fund & Execute Etch Order](https://docs.xverse.app/api/runes/etch-rune/create-rune-etch-order#next-step-greater-than-fund-and-execute-etch-order)

Estimate Rune Etch Order - Request[](https://docs.xverse.app/api/runes/etch-rune/estimate-rune-etch-order#estimate-rune-etch-order-request)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

You can specify the characteristics of the Runes Etch order you wish to estimate, by passing a request body, with the below properties:

Estimate Etch - Request Body

Description

`runeName`

a string representing the [name of the Rune](https://docs.ordinals.com/runes.html#name) to etch

`divisibility` (ℹ️ optional)

a number representing the rune's [divisibility](https://docs.ordinals.com/runes.html#divisibility)
, i.e. how finely it may be divided into its atomic units. Divisibility is expressed as the number of digits permissible after the decimal point in an amount of runes. The default value is 0.

`symbol` (ℹ️ optional)

a string representing the rune's currency [symbol](https://docs.ordinals.com/runes.html#symbol)
 as a single Unicode code point. If a rune does not have a symbol, the generic currency sign `¤`, also called a scarab, should be used

`premine` (ℹ️ optional)

a string representing rune [premine](https://docs.ordinals.com/runes.html#premine)
: the amount of rune units which the etcher can optionally allocate to themselves.

`isMintable`

a boolean indicating if the rune may have an open mint, allowing anyone to create and allocate units of that rune for themselves.

`terms` (ℹ️ optional)

an object describing the [terms](https://docs.ordinals.com/runes.html#terms)
 governing minting if the rune is mintable, with the below properties:

*   `amount` a string representing the fixed amount of new units of the rune that each mint transaction creates
    
*   `cap` a string representing the number of times a rune may be minted.
    
*   `heightStart` the mint is open starting in the block with the given start height. ℹ️Optional
    
*   `heightEnd` the rune may not be minted in or after the block with the given end height. ℹ️Optional
    
*   `offsetStart` the mint is open starting in the block whose height is equal to the start offset plus the height of the block in which the rune was etched. ℹ️Optional
    
*   `offsetEnd` the rune may not be minted in or after the block whose height is equal to the end offset plus the height of the block in which the rune was etched. ℹ️Optional
    

`inscriptionDetails` (ℹ️ optional)

You can optionally use the Rune etching transactions to inscribe some content for your user: - the inscription will be sent to the specified `destinationAddress`, along with the etched Runes - the etched Runes will be linked to the inscription If you wish to use that option, pass an `inscriptionDetails` object with the below properties to specify the content to inscribe:

*   `contentType` a string representing the content/mime type of the content being inscribed. Make sure you extract and pass the correct content type for the content you wish to inscribe
    
*   `contentBase64` a string representing the content you wish to inscribe, encoded in Base64 format
    

`delegateInscriptionId` (ℹ️ optional)

If you wish to use the Rune etching transactions to create an inscription, you can optionally nominate a [delegate](https://docs.ordinals.com/inscriptions/delegate.html)
 for your inscription, as an **alternative** to specifying its content with `inscriptionDetail` (see above)

You can **instead** choose to pass a `delegateInscriptionId` string representing the serialized binary inscription ID of the delegate.

`turbo` (ℹ️ optional)

a boolean marking the rune etching as opting into future rune protocol changes if set to `true` According to the [Runes specifications](https://docs.ordinals.com/runes/specification.html)
: _These protocol changes may increase light client validation costs, or just be highly degenerate_ ℹ️the default value is `false`

`destinationAddress`

The Bitcoin address to which the minted runes should be allocated. ⚠️ When minting Runes for your user, we recommend managing runes holdings on taproot/ordinal addresses.

`feeRate`

a number representing the desired fee rate to set for the Rune etch transactions, in sats per vbytes

`appServiceFee` (ℹ️ optional)

a number representing the sats value of the fee to charge your user for your service.

`appServiceFeeAddress` (ℹ️ optional)

a string representing the Bitcoin address which will receive the `appServiceFee`, if a fee is specified

`network` (ℹ️ optional)

a string representing the Bitcoin network to use for the etch transactions: `'Mainnet'` or `'Testnet'`

Estimate Rune Etch Order - Response[](https://docs.xverse.app/api/runes/etch-rune/estimate-rune-etch-order#estimate-rune-etch-order-response)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The response object is intended for you and your user to anticipate the costs of your specified etch order, with a clear breakdown:

Estimate Etch - Response Object

Description

`totalSize`

a number representing the total size of the etch transactions, in vbytes

`totalCost`

a number representing the total cost of the etch transactions in sats

`costBreakdown`

An object representing the cost breakdown of the etch transactions, with the below properties:

*   `postage` a number representing the total size of the Rune UTXOs etched, in sats
    
*   `networkFee` a number representing the total network fee for the etch transactions, in sats
    
*   `serviceFee` a number representing the total Sats Connect fee for processing the etch order, in sats
    
*   `appServiceFee` a number representing the optional service fee your app charges the user for the etch order
    

Next Step -> Create Mint Order[](https://docs.xverse.app/api/runes/etch-rune/estimate-rune-etch-order#next-step-greater-than-create-mint-order)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Use the [Create Etch Order](https://docs.xverse.app/api/runes/etch-rune/create-rune-etch-order)
 endpoint if you wish to proceed with a Runes Etch order after estimating its cost.

[NextCreate Rune Etch Order](https://docs.xverse.app/api/runes/etch-rune/create-rune-etch-order)

# Mint Rune | Xverse API | xverse

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://docs.xverse.app/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://docs.xverse.app/api/runes/mint-rune.md)
.

You can use Xverse API to mint Runes.

The Xverse API Runes Minting service lets you place a mint order for your app, and orchestrates the required transactions.

This documentation will guide you through the required steps:

1.  Estimate the cost of a Rune mint order-> see [Estimate Rune Mint Order](https://docs.xverse.app/api/runes/mint-rune/estimate-rune-mint-order)
    
2.  Create a rune mint order -> see [Create Rune Mint Order](https://docs.xverse.app/api/runes/mint-rune/create-rune-mint-order)
    
3.  Execute a Rune mint order -> see [Execute Rune Mint Order](https://docs.xverse.app/api/runes/mint-rune/execute-rune-mint-order)
    
4.  List and monitor Rune mint orders -> [List Rune Mint Orders](https://docs.xverse.app/api/runes/mint-rune/list-rune-mint-orders)
     & [Monitor Rune Mint Order](https://docs.xverse.app/api/runes/mint-rune/monitor-rune-mint-order)
    

[PreviousDecode Runestone](https://docs.xverse.app/api/runes/decode-runestone)
[NextEstimate Rune Mint Order](https://docs.xverse.app/api/runes/mint-rune/estimate-rune-mint-order)

Last updated 10 months ago

# Verify Bitcoin message signatures | Sats Connect | xverse

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://docs.xverse.app/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://docs.xverse.app/sats-connect/guides/verify-bitcoin-message-signatures.md)
.

Signature verification depends on the type of address the message was signed with

### Ordinals Address `P2TR`[](https://docs.xverse.app/sats-connect/guides/verify-bitcoin-message-signatures#ordinals-address-p2tr)

Message signing for P2TR addresses conform to the BIP-322 standard. The following options are available to verify the message signature.

*   Calling the `verifymessage` RPC command on a Bitcoin node built from the source of the unreleased BIP-322 feature branch. Reference: [https://developer.bitcoin.org/reference/rpc/verifymessage.html](https://developer.bitcoin.org/reference/rpc/verifymessage.html)
     [https://github.com/bitcoin/bitcoin/pull/24058](https://github.com/bitcoin/bitcoin/pull/24058)
    
*   Use the NPM package BIP322-js [https://www.npmjs.com/package/bip322-js](https://www.npmjs.com/package/bip322-js)
    

ℹ️ Note that due to the experimental nature of BIP-322 none of the above options are officially endorsed or audited. Use at your own risk.

### Payment Address `P2SH(P2WPKH)`[](https://docs.xverse.app/sats-connect/guides/verify-bitcoin-message-signatures#payment-address-p2sh-p2wpkh)

Messages from the payment address are signed with an ECDSA signature, conforming to the standard described here: [https://en.bitcoin.it/wiki/Message\_signing](https://en.bitcoin.it/wiki/Message_signing)

You can decode the signed message and use a cryptographic library to verify the signature.

Using `bitcoinjs-message` can also be a convenient option if you're working within a JavaScript or Node.js environment. It abstracts away the lower-level details of ECDSA verification and the Bitcoin message signing standard, allowing you to verify signed messages with minimal code:

#### Step 1: Install bitcoinjs-message[](https://docs.xverse.app/sats-connect/guides/verify-bitcoin-message-signatures#step-1-install-bitcoinjs-message)

First, you need to install the `bitcoinjs-message` library. If you're using npm, you can install it by running:

Copy

    npm install bitcoinjs-message

#### Step 2: Verify the Message[](https://docs.xverse.app/sats-connect/guides/verify-bitcoin-message-signatures#step-2-verify-the-message)

Once installed, you can use the library to verify a message with just a few lines of code. Here's a simple example:

ℹ️ Note that none of the above options are officially endorsed or audited. Use at your own risk.

[PreviousSpark BTKN Airdrops](https://docs.xverse.app/sats-connect/spark-methods/spark-btkn-airdrops)
[NextCreating Bitcoin PSBTs](https://docs.xverse.app/sats-connect/guides/creating-bitcoin-psbts)

*   [Ordinals Address P2TR](https://docs.xverse.app/sats-connect/guides/verify-bitcoin-message-signatures#ordinals-address-p2tr)
    
*   [Payment Address P2SH(P2WPKH)](https://docs.xverse.app/sats-connect/guides/verify-bitcoin-message-signatures#payment-address-p2sh-p2wpkh)
    

Copy

    const bitcoinMessage = require('bitcoinjs-message');
    
    // The message that was signed
    const message = "This is a test message";
    
    // The signature in base64 format
    const signature = "H8yAIxMVQfVcY4J//yA8hI3Fy2kL...base64signature...";
    
    // The Bitcoin address of the signer
    const address = "1ExampleOnlyDoNotSendToThisAddressDoNotTryToDecodeIt";
    
    // Verify the message
    const isValid = bitcoinMessage.verify(message, address, signature);
    
    console.log("Is the signature valid?", isValid);

# Mobile Integration | Sats Connect | xverse

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://docs.xverse.app/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://docs.xverse.app/sats-connect/guides/mobile-integration.md)
.

To have access to sats-connect methods on mobile you need to direct your users to the Xverse in-app browser you can do so using the following methods

### **1\. Custom URL Scheme**[](https://docs.xverse.app/sats-connect/guides/mobile-integration#id-1.-custom-url-scheme)

Xverse provides a custom URL scheme for deep linking:

Copy

    xverse://

#### **1.1. Opening the In-App Browser**[](https://docs.xverse.app/sats-connect/guides/mobile-integration#id-1.1.-opening-the-in-app-browser)

To open the in-app browser of Xverse, use the following link:

Copy

    xverse://browser

#### **1.2. Navigating to a Specific URL in the In-App Browser**[](https://docs.xverse.app/sats-connect/guides/mobile-integration#id-1.2.-navigating-to-a-specific-url-in-the-in-app-browser)

To open a specific URL in the in-app browser of Xverse, use the following format:

Copy

    xverse://browser?url=YOUR_URL_HERE

For example, to open `www.magiceden.io` in the in-app browser, use:

Copy

    xverse://browser?url=www.magiceden.io

**Note**: The custom URL scheme for Xverse is deprecated. Instead, it's advised to utilize Universal Links for iOS and Application Links for Android when linking with Xverse.

### **2\. Universal Links and Application Links**[](https://docs.xverse.app/sats-connect/guides/mobile-integration#id-2.-universal-links-and-application-links)

For enhanced security, Xverse utilizes Universal Links on iOS and Application Links on Android.

#### **2.1. iOS: Universal Links**[](https://docs.xverse.app/sats-connect/guides/mobile-integration#id-2.1.-ios-universal-links)

To link to Xverse using Universal Links on iOS, use the following format:

To open the in-app browser of Xverse, use the following link:

For instance, to navigate to `www.gamma.io` in the in-app browser, use:

The only deep linking parameter currently supported by Xverse is `browser`. To navigate to the in-app browser, include the `browser` parameter in your link.

#### **2.2. Android: Application Links**[](https://docs.xverse.app/sats-connect/guides/mobile-integration#id-2.2.-android-application-links)

On Android, Xverse utilizes Application Links, which function similarly to iOS's Universal Links. To deep link into Xverse on Android, use the same format as for iOS:

Opening this link will launch the Xverse app and navigate to the browser screen inside the app.

# Etch Rune | Xverse API | xverse

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://docs.xverse.app/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://docs.xverse.app/api/runes/etch-rune.md)
.

You can use Xverse API to etch Runes.

The Xverse API runes etching service lets you place an etch order for your app, and orchestrates the required transactions.

This documentation will guide you through the required steps:

1.  Validate you rune name & estimate the cost of a Rune Etch order-> see [Validate Rune Name](https://docs.xverse.app/api/runes/etch-rune/validate-rune-name)
    & [Estimate Rune Etch Order](https://docs.xverse.app/api/runes/etch-rune/estimate-rune-etch-order)
    
2.  Create a Rune Etch order -> see [Create Rune Etch Order](https://docs.xverse.app/api/runes/etch-rune/create-rune-etch-order)
    
3.  Execute a Rune Etch order -> see [Execute Rune Etch Order](https://docs.xverse.app/api/runes/etch-rune/execute-rune-etch-order)
    
4.  List and monitor Rune Etch orders -> [List Rune Etch Orders](https://docs.xverse.app/api/runes/etch-rune/list-rune-etch-orders)
     &[Monitor Rune Etch Order](https://docs.xverse.app/api/runes/etch-rune/monitor-rune-etch-order)

# Mint Runes | Sats Connect | xverse

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://docs.xverse.app/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://docs.xverse.app/sats-connect/bitcoin-methods/mint-runes.md)
.

You can use Sats Connect to let your users mint [Runes](https://docs.ordinals.com/runes.html)
 through your app, using their wallet.

Sats Connect's Runes minting service lets you place a mint order on behalf of your user, and orchestrates the required transactions.

This documentation will guide you through the required steps:

1.  Estimate the cost of a Rune mint order for your user -> see [runes\_estimateMint](https://docs.xverse.app/sats-connect/bitcoin-methods/mint-runes/runes_estimatemint)
    
2.  Execute a Rune mint order, and prompt your user to sign the funding transaction required to process the order -> see [runes\_mint](https://docs.xverse.app/sats-connect/bitcoin-methods/mint-runes/runes_mint)
    
3.  Fetch the status of a Rune mint order -> see [runes\_getOrder](https://docs.xverse.app/sats-connect/bitcoin-methods/runes_getorder)
    

[Previousrunes\_transfer](https://docs.xverse.app/sats-connect/bitcoin-methods/runes_transfer)
[Nextrunes\_estimateMint](https://docs.xverse.app/sats-connect/bitcoin-methods/mint-runes/runes_estimatemint)

# Speed up a Rune Mint or Etch order | Sats Connect | xverse

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://docs.xverse.app/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://docs.xverse.app/sats-connect/bitcoin-methods/speed-up-a-rune-mint-or-etch-order.md)
.

Bitcoin fee rates can fluctuate quickly when the network is congested. This can cause the transactions involved in your [Rune mint order](https://docs.xverse.app/sats-connect/bitcoin-methods/mint-runes) or [Rune etch order](https://docs.xverse.app/sats-connect/bitcoin-methods/etch-runes)
 to remain stuck in the mempool, if the fee rate initially specified for your order has become too low for the present network conditions. You can use Sats Connect to speed up an order, using the [Replace-by-Fee (RBF)](https://www.xverse.app/blog/replace-by-fee-rbf)
 mechanism. Sats Connect will replace the stuck transactions with new transactions broadcast at a higher fee rate.

This documentation will guide you through the required steps:

1.  Estimate the cost of a speeding up a Rune mint or etch order for your user -> see [runes\_estimateRbfOrder](https://docs.xverse.app/sats-connect/bitcoin-methods/speed-up-a-rune-mint-or-etch-order/runes_estimaterbforder)
    
2.  Speed up your Rune mint or etch order, and prompt your user to sign the funding transaction required to process the order at an increased fee rate -> see [runes\_rbfOrder](https://docs.xverse.app/sats-connect/bitcoin-methods/speed-up-a-rune-mint-or-etch-order/runes_rbforder)
    
3.  Fetch the new status of a Rune mint or etch order -> see [runes\_getOrder](https://docs.xverse.app/sats-connect/bitcoin-methods/runes_getorder)
    

[Previousrunes\_getOrder](https://docs.xverse.app/sats-connect/bitcoin-methods/runes_getorder)
[Nextrunes\_estimateRbfOrder](https://docs.xverse.app/sats-connect/bitcoin-methods/speed-up-a-rune-mint-or-etch-order/runes_estimaterbforder)

Last updated 2 years ago

# ord_sendInscriptions | Sats Connect | xverse

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://docs.xverse.app/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://docs.xverse.app/sats-connect/bitcoin-methods/ord_sendinscriptions.md)
.

You can use the `ord_sendInscriptions` method to request inscription transfers from your user's [connected wallet's ordinal address](https://docs.xverse.app/sats-connect/connecting-to-the-wallet)
. You can request multiple transfers in a single request, each transfer involving:

*   an inscription
    
*   a recipient address
    

The method accepts a `transfers` array as request parameter:

Request parameters

Description

`transfers`

an array of transfer objects with <`inscriptionId`, `recipient`\> properties:

*   `inscriptionId` - string: the [id](https://docs.ordinals.com/inscriptions.html#inscription-ids)
     of the inscription. ℹ️ You can use the [`ord_getInscriptions`](https://docs.xverse.app/sats-connect/bitcoin-methods/ord_getinscriptions)
     method to retrieve your user's inscriptions
    
*   `address` - string: the Bitcoin address of the inscription transfer recipient ⚠️ We recommend managing inscriptions on taproot/ordinal addresses.
    

Copy

    import { request } from "sats-connect";
    
       const response = await request('ord_sendInscriptions', {transfers: 
        [\
          {\
            inscriptionId: '22e63b19dede2882b2478f7182aa4ea8cd4a8289acd7a7d5dde96fcaaf4cd089i0',\
            address: ordinalsAddress1,\
          },\
          {\
            inscriptionId: '9a83420dbe566531114066a03d8022425064a78992710fab7006948eaa1d3546i0',\
            address: ordinalsAddress2,\
          }]  
        });
    
        if (response.status === "success") {
          // handle success
        } else {
          if (response.error.code === RpcErrorCode.USER_REJECTION) {
            // handle user cancellation error
          } else {
            // handle error
          }
        }
      } catch (err) {
          alert(err.error.message);
      }

The user will be prompted to review the inscription transfer transaction in the wallet, and to confirm:

*   the inscriptions to send and their recipients
    
*   their desired Bitcoin transaction fee
    

The transaction will be signed and broadcasted upon user approval.

The `ord_sendInscriptions` method returns a Promise that resolves to the `sendInscriptionsResultSchema` object:

Property

Description

`txid`

The Bitcoin transaction id as a hex-encoded string.

[Previousord\_getInscriptions](https://docs.xverse.app/sats-connect/bitcoin-methods/ord_getinscriptions)
[Nextrunes\_getBalance](https://docs.xverse.app/sats-connect/bitcoin-methods/runes_getbalance)

Last updated 8 months ago

# stx_transferStx | Sats Connect | xverse

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://docs.xverse.app/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://docs.xverse.app/sats-connect/stacks-methods/stx_transferstx.md)
.

You can use the `stx_transferStx` method to request a transfer of any amount of Stacks to any recipient from the user's wallet.

Request parameters

Description

`amount`

a string representing the amount of STX tokens to transfer, in microstacks units. (Anything parseable by `BigInt` is acceptable)

`recipient`

a string representing the recipient's Crockford base-32 encoded Stacks address

`memo`

ℹ️ Optional a string representing the transaction memo

Copy

      import { request } from "sats-connect";
      
      try {
          const response = await request("stx_transferStx", {
            recipient,
            amount: Number(amount),
            memo,
          });
          if ("result" in response) {
            alert(response.result.txid);
          } else {
            alert(response.error.message);
          }
        } catch (error) {
          console.error(error);
          alert(error.message);
        }
      };

The user will be prompted to review the Stacks transfer transaction in the wallet, with the recipient, memo and amount to send. Xverse browser extension UI shown as example:

![](https://docs.xverse.app/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fcontent.gitbook.com%2Fcontent%2F33DLypUqgcjkBSmN0gZn%2Fblobs%2FKxLPikP6g8V3ktgpBEdn%2Fimage.png&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=4784c6ac76f1bd219f087a05fb383e57&sv=3)

The transaction will be signed and broadcasted upon user approval.

The `stx_transferStx` method returns a Promise that resolves to the `TransferStxResult` object:

Property

Description

`txid`

a hex-encoded string representing the ID of the transfer STX transaction signed

`transaction`

a hex-encoded string reperesenting the transfer STX transaction signed

[Previousstx\_signStructuredMessage](https://docs.xverse.app/sats-connect/stacks-methods/stx_signstructuredmessage)
[Nextstx\_signTransaction](https://docs.xverse.app/sats-connect/stacks-methods/stx_signtransaction)

# spark_transfer | Sats Connect | xverse

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://docs.xverse.app/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://docs.xverse.app/sats-connect/spark-methods/spark_transfer.md)
.

You can use the `spark_transfer` method to send **Spark BTC** from the user’s wallet to any valid Spark address.

Parameters[](https://docs.xverse.app/sats-connect/spark-methods/spark_transfer#parameters)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Request parameters

Description

`reciverSparkAddress`

a string representing the destination Spark address Example: "sprt1pgssyuuuhnrrdjswal5c3s3rafw9w3y5dd4cjy3duxlf7hjzkp0rqx6dj6mrhu"

`amountSats`

a string representing the amount of BTC to transfer, in satoshis.

Example[](https://docs.xverse.app/sats-connect/spark-methods/spark_transfer#example)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Copy

    import { request } from "sats-connect";
    
    try {
      const response = await request("spark_transfer", {
        receiverSparkAddress: "sprt1pgssyuuuhnrrdjswal5c3s3rafw9w3y5dd4cjy3duxlf7hjzkp0rqx6dj6mrhu",
        amountSats: 100, // Amount to send, in satoshis
      });
    
      if ("result" in response) {
        console.log("Spark transfer successful:", response.result.txid);
      } else {
        console.error("Spark transfer failed:", response.error.message);
      }
    } catch (error) {
      console.error("Unexpected error:", error);
    }
    

What `spark_transfer` does[](https://docs.xverse.app/sats-connect/spark-methods/spark_transfer#what-spark_transfer-does)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Calling this method prompts the user to:

1.  Review the **recipient address** and **amount** of Spark BTC to send.
    
2.  **Approve the transaction** in their Xverse wallet.
    
3.  Upon approval, the transaction is **signed and broadcasted** to the Spark network.
    

The `spark_transfer` method returns a Promise that resolves to a `SparkTransferResult` :

Property

Description

`txid`

a hex-encoded string representing the ID of the broadcasted BTC transfer transaction on Spark

[Previousspark\_getBalance](https://docs.xverse.app/sats-connect/spark-methods/spark_getbalance)
[Nextspark\_transferToken](https://docs.xverse.app/sats-connect/spark-methods/spark_transfertoken)

Last updated 8 months ago
